Um, enjoying things? Well, I mean, I try to. I really do. It's just... I guess I haven't had as much time for my usual hobbies lately. 

Normally, I love playing golf on the weekends or, you know, just tinkering in the garage. But since they moved in, I feel like I should be present, you know? Like it would be rude to just disappear for four hours on a Saturday. So I guess I haven't been doing those things as much. 

But I'm sure I'd still enjoy them if I did them! I don't want it to sound like I've lost interest in life or anything dramatic like that. I'm just... prioritizing family time right now. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? Putting family first?

Um, avoiding social situations? Oh, no, definitely not avoiding. I mean, I wouldn't want you to think I'm hiding away or becoming a hermit or anything.

Um, irritable? Oh, I wouldn't say *most* of the time. I mean, I don't want to sound like some angry guy stomping around the house. I try really hard to keep the peace. 

But... well, I guess I have been a little more, um, keyed up lately? It's just that I've been feeling so worn down with everything going on. And... I suppose I have been snapping at my wife a bit more than I should. I feel awful about it, I really do. It's just the stress, you know? 

And it's sort of spilling over into my work, too, which isn't like me at all. I'm a manager for artists and performers, so I usually pride myself on being on top of things. But lately, I've been avoiding calls I'd normally handle right away. I even... well, I nearly let a musician's contract fall apart the other day because I just couldn't think straight. 

It's embarrassing to admit that. I just want you to know I'm usually much better at handling pressure. It's just this specific situation with the house being so full... anyone would be a little on edge in my shoes, wouldn't they? I'm just trying my best to keep it all together.

While the patient mentioned not being able to think straight and nearly letting a contract fall apart in the previous turn (which is part of the stress-related maladaptive response and functional impairment), there is no specific symptom profile item exclusively for 'trouble focusing/concentration' (like in MDD or GAD profiles). The closest items are S081 (which was already addressed in the previous turn regarding irritability/snapping/work issues) and functional impairment. Since the specific symptom of concentration difficulty as a standalone question isn't mapped to a distinct item in the provided Adjustment Disorder profile (other than what was already covered in S081), 'None' is appropriate."}

Trouble focusing? Um, no, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble focusing. I mean, I know I just mentioned that thing with the contract, but that was really just a one-off. A blip, you know?

Worrying about the future? Um, no, I wouldn't really say I do that. I mean, obviously, in my line of work, I have to plan ahead for my clients, but that's just normal career stuff, not... not like anxious worrying. 

I actually try to stay really optimistic. Even with things being so crowded and, well, a bit heavy at home right now, I don't sit around dwelling on worst-case scenarios. I think it's really important to keep a positive outlook, you know? If I started expecting things to go wrong, I wouldn't be much help to anyone. Oh, definitely. Um, I mean, I know I don't have a ton of free time right now, what with making sure my wife and her parents are all settled and taken care of. But when I do get a second to myself, I still really enjoy things.

Well, I mean, I try to. I really do. It's just... My wife has a lot on her plate with her parents being there, and I'd feel terrible leaving her to manage everything while I'm out having a good time. \n\nSo, I guess I haven't been seeing my friends as much, but it's not because I'm avoiding them. I don't want you to write down that I'm depressed or losing my spark or anything like that. I'm just adapting to the current living situation, which is what any responsible adult would do, right? My wife's mom is actually a wonderful cook, so we've been having these nice, big family dinners every night. And, um, work has been... well, I'm a talent manager, and I've found myself avoiding calls that I would normally just handle immediately. I even... I'm a little embarrassed to admit this, but I almost let a musician's contract fall apart the other day because my head was just so foggy. I just couldn't think straight. \n\nDoes that... does that make sense as a reason to be here? I just want to get back to being myself and doing a good job for everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, sad? No, I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad, exactly. I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or ungrateful, because I'm really glad we can help them out. It's just... more of a tired feeling, you know? Like I just don't have the energy I usually do. \n\nI definitely haven't been crying or anything like that. Just... maybe a little drained? I hope that's a normal way to react to a big change like this. Is it normal to just feel a bit... worn out, rather than actually sad?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? I don't... I don't think I'd use that word, no. I know I said I was feeling a little \"keyed up\" earlier, but it's not like I'm sitting around worrying all the time or anything like that. I just... well, when you have extra people in your space, you want to make sure everyone is comfortable, right? It's more like this constant pressure to be a good host and keep things running smoothly for my wife and her parents. \n\nI really hope I don't sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. It's just an adjustment, you know, having to always be \"on\" at home after being \"on\" at work all day. Does that make sense? I wouldn't want you to think I'm overly anxious or neurotic, I'm really just... trying to make sure everyone is happy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, enjoying things? Well, I mean, I try to. I really do. It's just... I guess I haven't had as much time for my usual hobbies lately. \n\nNormally, I love playing golf on the weekends or, you know, just tinkering in the garage. But since they moved in, I feel like I should be present, you know? Like it would be rude to just disappear for four hours on a Saturday. So I guess I haven't been doing those things as much. \n\nBut I'm sure I'd still enjoy them if I did them! I don't want it to sound like I've lost interest in life or anything dramatic like that. I'm just... prioritizing family time right now. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? Putting family first?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any social situations since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, avoiding social situations? Oh, no, definitely not avoiding. I mean, I wouldn't want you to think I'm hiding away or becoming a hermit or anything. \n\nIt's more just... well, with everyone at the house, it feels a bit complicated to have friends over right now. And going out... I just feel like I should be home, helping out, you know? My wife has a lot on her plate with her parents being there, and I'd feel terrible leaving her to manage everything while I'm out having a good time. \n\nSo, I guess I haven't been seeing my friends as much, but it's not because I'm avoiding them. It's just... being supportive of the current situation at home. That's a good reason to stay in, right? I just want to be a supportive husband.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, less interested? Well, I think we sort of touched on this a second ago, with the golf and the garage stuff. I wouldn't say I'm *less interested* in those things at all. I still love them! \n\nIt's really just a matter of time and, well, being considerate of the guests in our home. I mean, if I had a free afternoon and the house was empty, I'd be out on the course in a heartbeat. But right now, I just feel like my focus needs to be on my wife and her parents. \n\nI'm still the same guy with the same interests, I promise. I just... I don't want you to write down that I'm depressed or losing my spark or anything like that. I'm just adapting to the current living situation, which is what any responsible adult would do, right? I'm just trying to do the right thing for my family.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, my weight? Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really been checking the scale or anything, but my clothes all still fit exactly the same. \n\nI know sometimes people, you know, stress-eat or stop eating when things get busy at home, but I've been making sure to keep up with regular meals. My wife's mom is actually a wonderful cook, so we've been having these nice, big family dinners every night. I definitely wouldn't want to offend her by not eating! \n\nSo, yeah, my weight is perfectly stable. I'm taking good care of myself in that department. That's a good sign, right? That I'm handling everything okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable most of the time since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, irritable? Oh, I wouldn't say *most* of the time. I mean, I don't want to sound like some angry guy stomping around the house. I try really hard to keep the peace. \n\nBut... well, I guess I have been a little more, um, keyed up lately? It's just that I've been feeling so worn down with everything going on. And... I suppose I have been snapping at my wife a bit more than I should. I feel awful about it, I really do. It's just the stress, you know? \n\nAnd it's sort of spilling over into my work, too, which isn't like me at all. I'm a manager for artists and performers, so I usually pride myself on being on top of things. But lately, I've been avoiding calls I'd normally handle right away. I even... well, I nearly let a musician's contract fall apart the other day because I just couldn't think straight. \n\nIt's embarrassing to admit that. I just want you to know I'm usually much better at handling pressure. It's just this specific situation with the house being so full... anyone would be a little on edge in my shoes, wouldn't they? I'm just trying my best to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing on things since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Trouble focusing? Um, no, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble focusing. I mean, I know I just mentioned that thing with the contract, but that was really just a one-off. A blip, you know? \n\nGenerally speaking, my concentration is totally fine. I can sit down and read a brief or review a schedule just as well as I always could. I'm still getting my work done, I promise. It's just that I'm, well, maybe a little distracted sometimes because I'm trying to make sure everyone at home is comfortable and happy. That's a good thing, though, right? Being attentive to your family? \n\nI definitely don't have ADD or anything like that. My mind is sharp. I just... I have a lot on my plate right now, and I'm just trying to balance it all. I think I'm doing a pretty decent job of keeping my eye on the ball, all things considered. Don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about the future or things that might go wrong?\n\nPatient: Worrying about the future? Um, no, I wouldn't really say I do that. I mean, obviously, in my line of work, I have to plan ahead for my clients, but that's just normal career stuff, not... not like anxious worrying. \n\nI actually try to stay really optimistic. Even with things being so crowded and, well, a bit heavy at home right now, I don't sit around dwelling on worst-case scenarios. I just focus on taking care of my wife and her parents, making sure everyone has what they need and that everyone is comfortable. \n\nI think it's really important to keep a positive outlook, you know? If I started expecting things to go wrong, I wouldn't be much help to anyone. So I just keep pushing forward and looking on the bright side. That's a healthy way to approach it, right? Just staying positive and taking it day by day?\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the activities you're managing to fit in, even if it's less often than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, definitely. Um, I mean, I know I don't have a ton of free time right now, what with making sure my wife and her parents are all settled and taken care of. But when I do get a second to myself, I still really enjoy things. \n\nLike, if I'm listening to a client's new track or just catching a few innings of a game on TV, I still get a lot of enjoyment out of it. I'm definitely not sitting around feeling numb or anything like that. I haven't lost my spark. \n\nI think... well, I think it's really important to appreciate those little moments, especially when life gets a bit crowded and busy. It shows I'm still keeping a good balance, right? I mean, you'd agree that's a really good sign, wouldn't you?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now don't enjoy as much since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I think I still enjoy all the same things I used to. \n\nObviously, I don't get to do them as often right now, just because I'm trying to be really present at home and make sure everyone is, you know, comfortable and getting along. But that's just a matter of scheduling, not a lack of interest. \n\nIf I had the time to go to a nice dinner or play a round of golf, I know I'd still love it just as much. I'm not... I haven't lost my passion for things. I'm just prioritizing my family right now, which is what you're supposed to do, right? Putting family first is a good thing, don't you think?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Sad? Um, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, sure, it’s a big adjustment having a full house right now, but I don't feel... down or anything like that. 

When I’m doing things I enjoy, I’m genuinely enjoying them. I really try to keep a smile on my face, especially for my wife and her parents. It’s important to set a good tone, you know? I’m not the type to just sit around and mope anyway. I like to stay upbeat and keep everyone else’s spirits up, too. That’s the best way to handle a tricky situation, isn't it? Just keeping your chin up and staying strong for your family?

Um, no, not really. I mean, sure, I have the usual things on my mind—like making sure the house is running smoothly and that my wife is feeling supported with her parents here—but I wouldn't call them, like, repetitive worries or anything.

I definitely don't let myself get stuck on negative thoughts. I prefer to just focus on what I can do to help and keep things positive. That's a healthy way to manage a busy household, wouldn't you agree?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Anxious? Oh, no. No, definitely not most of the day, and certainly not every day. 

I mean, I think anyone in my position would feel a little... I don't know, a little keyed up sometimes? Just wanting to make sure everything is perfect for the in-laws and that my wife isn't getting too stressed. But it's not anxiety, it's just... being conscientious, I guess.

I'm usually pretty good at just taking a deep breath and handling whatever comes up. That's the right way to approach it, isn't it? Just staying calm and collected for everyone else's sake?

I guess sometimes I wonder if I could have, like, planned a nicer dinner or maybe helped my wife a little more with the grocery shopping... but that's just wanting to be a good host and a good husband. It's not guilt. I just want everything to be nice for them. 

I really try to make sure I don't drop the ball on anything, especially with work being so demanding right now. I just want to make sure everyone is happy and taken care of. That's a good thing, right? Being mindful of how your actions affect your family?

Trouble calming down? Um, no, I wouldn't say so.

I don't think I'd use that word, no. I know I said I was feeling a little \"keyed up\" earlier, but it's not like I'm sitting around worrying all the time or anything like that. I just... well, when you have extra people in your space, you want to make sure everyone is comfortable, right? It's more like this constant pressure to be a good host and keep things running smoothly for my wife and her parents. \n\nI really hope I don't sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. It's just an adjustment, you know, having to always be \"on\" at home after being \"on\" at work all day. Does that make sense? I wouldn't want you to think I'm overly anxious or neurotic, I'm really just... trying to make sure everyone is happy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, enjoying things? Well, I mean, I try to. I really do. It's just... I guess I haven't had as much time for my usual hobbies lately. \n\nNormally, I love playing golf on the weekends or, you know, just tinkering in the garage. But since they moved in, I feel like I should be present, you know? Like it would be rude to just disappear for four hours on a Saturday. So I guess I haven't been doing those things as much. \n\nBut I'm sure I'd still enjoy them if I did them! I don't want it to sound like I've lost interest in life or anything dramatic like that. I'm just... prioritizing family time right now. That's the right thing to do, isn't it? Putting family first?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any social situations since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, avoiding social situations? Oh, no, definitely not avoiding. I mean, I wouldn't want you to think I'm hiding away or becoming a hermit or anything. \n\nIt's more just... well, with everyone at the house, it feels a bit complicated to have friends over right now. And going out... I just feel like I should be home, helping out, you know? My wife has a lot on her plate with her parents being there, and I'd feel terrible leaving her to manage everything while I'm out having a good time. \n\nSo, I guess I haven't been seeing my friends as much, but it's not because I'm avoiding them. It's just... being supportive of the current situation at home. That's a good reason to stay in, right? I just want to be a supportive husband.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, less interested? Well, I think we sort of touched on this a second ago, with the golf and the garage stuff. I wouldn't say I'm *less interested* in those things at all. I still love them! \n\nIt's really just a matter of time and, well, being considerate of the guests in our home. I mean, if I had a free afternoon and the house was empty, I'd be out on the course in a heartbeat. But right now, I just feel like my focus needs to be on my wife and her parents. \n\nI'm still the same guy with the same interests, I promise. I just... I don't want you to write down that I'm depressed or losing my spark or anything like that. I'm just adapting to the current living situation, which is what any responsible adult would do, right? I'm just trying to do the right thing for my family.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, my weight? Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really been checking the scale or anything, but my clothes all still fit exactly the same. \n\nI know sometimes people, you know, stress-eat or stop eating when things get busy at home, but I've been making sure to keep up with regular meals. My wife's mom is actually a wonderful cook, so we've been having these nice, big family dinners every night. I definitely wouldn't want to offend her by not eating! \n\nSo, yeah, my weight is perfectly stable. I'm taking good care of myself in that department. That's a good sign, right? That I'm handling everything okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable most of the time since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, irritable? Oh, I wouldn't say *most* of the time. I mean, I don't want to sound like some angry guy stomping around the house. I try really hard to keep the peace. \n\nBut... well, I guess I have been a little more, um, keyed up lately? It's just that I've been feeling so worn down with everything going on. And... I suppose I have been snapping at my wife a bit more than I should. I feel awful about it, I really do. It's just the stress, you know? \n\nAnd it's sort of spilling over into my work, too, which isn't like me at all. I'm a manager for artists and performers, so I usually pride myself on being on top of things. But lately, I've been avoiding calls I'd normally handle right away. I even... well, I nearly let a musician's contract fall apart the other day because I just couldn't think straight. \n\nIt's embarrassing to admit that. I just want you to know I'm usually much better at handling pressure. It's just this specific situation with the house being so full... anyone would be a little on edge in my shoes, wouldn't they? I'm just trying my best to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing on things since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Trouble focusing? Um, no, I wouldn't say I'm having trouble focusing. I mean, I know I just mentioned that thing with the contract, but that was really just a one-off. A blip, you know? \n\nGenerally speaking, my concentration is totally fine. I can sit down and read a brief or review a schedule just as well as I always could. I'm still getting my work done, I promise. It's just that I'm, well, maybe a little distracted sometimes because I'm trying to make sure everyone at home is comfortable and happy. That's a good thing, though, right? Being attentive to your family? \n\nI definitely don't have ADD or anything like that. My mind is sharp. I just... I have a lot on my plate right now, and I'm just trying to balance it all. I think I'm doing a pretty decent job of keeping my eye on the ball, all things considered. Don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about the future or things that might go wrong?\n\nPatient: Worrying about the future? Um, no, I wouldn't really say I do that. I mean, obviously, in my line of work, I have to plan ahead for my clients, but that's just normal career stuff, not... not like anxious worrying. \n\nI actually try to stay really optimistic. Even with things being so crowded and, well, a bit heavy at home right now, I don't sit around dwelling on worst-case scenarios. I just focus on taking care of my wife and her parents, making sure everyone has what they need and that everyone is comfortable. \n\nI think it's really important to keep a positive outlook, you know? If I started expecting things to go wrong, I wouldn't be much help to anyone. So I just keep pushing forward and looking on the bright side. That's a healthy way to approach it, right? Just staying positive and taking it day by day?\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the activities you're managing to fit in, even if it's less often than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, definitely. Um, I mean, I know I don't have a ton of free time right now, what with making sure my wife and her parents are all settled and taken care of. But when I do get a second to myself, I still really enjoy things. \n\nLike, if I'm listening to a client's new track or just catching a few innings of a game on TV, I still get a lot of enjoyment out of it. I'm definitely not sitting around feeling numb or anything like that. I haven't lost my spark. \n\nI think... well, I think it's really important to appreciate those little moments, especially when life gets a bit crowded and busy. It shows I'm still keeping a good balance, right? I mean, you'd agree that's a really good sign, wouldn't you?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now don't enjoy as much since your in-laws moved in?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I think I still enjoy all the same things I used to. \n\nObviously, I don't get to do them as often right now, just because I'm trying to be really present at home and make sure everyone is, you know, comfortable and getting along. But that's just a matter of scheduling, not a lack of interest. \n\nIf I had the time to go to a nice dinner or play a round of golf, I know I'd still love it just as much. I'm not... I haven't lost my passion for things. I'm just prioritizing my family right now, which is what you're supposed to do, right? Putting family first is a good thing, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time, even when you're doing things that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, sure, it’s a big adjustment having a full house right now, but I don't feel... down or anything like that. \n\nWhen I’m doing things I enjoy, I’m genuinely enjoying them. I really try to keep a smile on my face, especially for my wife and her parents. It’s important to set a good tone, you know? If I'm walking around looking sad, it's just going to make them feel like a burden, and I definitely don't want that. \n\nI’m not the type to just sit around and mope anyway. I like to stay upbeat and keep everyone else’s spirits up, too. That’s the best way to handle a tricky situation, isn't it? Just keeping your chin up and staying strong for your family?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the same worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, sure, I have the usual things on my mind—like making sure the house is running smoothly and that my wife is feeling supported with her parents here—but I wouldn't call them, like, repetitive worries or anything. \n\nI'm definitely not obsessing over things. I just try to stay on top of the logistics, you know? It's just being responsible and making sure everyone's needs are met. I think it's pretty normal to keep a mental checklist when you've got a lot of moving parts in your household, right? \n\nI definitely don't let myself get stuck on negative thoughts. I prefer to just focus on what I can do to help and keep things positive. That's a healthy way to manage a busy household, wouldn't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Oh, no. No, definitely not most of the day, and certainly not every day. \n\nI mean, I think anyone in my position would feel a little... I don't know, a little keyed up sometimes? Just wanting to make sure everything is perfect for the in-laws and that my wife isn't getting too stressed. But it's not anxiety, it's just... being conscientious, I guess. \n\nI really try to keep a calm environment, you know? If I'm walking around anxious all day, that's just going to rub off on everyone else, and I'd hate to be the cause of any tension in the house. I'm usually pretty good at just taking a deep breath and handling whatever comes up. That's the right way to approach it, isn't it? Just staying calm and collected for everyone else's sake?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most days?\n\nPatient: Um, I think you might have asked something a bit like this earlier, but yeah, no... I really don't feel sad most days. \n\nI mean, life is just way too busy right now to sit around feeling down, you know? I really make a point to focus on the positives. Like I was saying, it's just so important to me to project a good attitude for everyone else in the house. If I started moping around or acting sad, my wife's parents might think they're overstaying their welcome, and I would feel absolutely terrible if I made them feel that way. \n\nSo, no, definitely not sad. Just... focused on keeping the peace and making sure everyone is comfortable, I suppose. That's the right way to handle it when you're hosting family, right? Just putting their needs first?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I wouldn't say I feel guilty. I mean, I try my best to do everything I can for everyone, so there's not really much to feel guilty about, you know? \n\nI guess sometimes I wonder if I could have, like, planned a nicer dinner or maybe helped my wife a little more with the grocery shopping... but that's just wanting to be a good host and a good husband. It's not guilt. I just want everything to be nice for them. \n\nI really try to make sure I don't drop the ball on anything, especially with work being so demanding right now. I just want to make sure everyone is happy and taken care of. That's a good thing, right? Being mindful of how your actions affect your family?\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble calming down after you feel upset or anxious?\n\nPatient: Trouble calming down? Um, no, I wouldn't say so. \n\nIf something... you know, if there's a little hiccup or a frustrating moment with work or around the house, I usually just bounce right back. I mean, dwelling on things doesn't help anyone, right? I just take a quick breather, maybe get a glass of water, and get right back to normal. \n\nI'd really hate to ruin the mood for my wife or her parents by acting flustered or letting something get to me. It's much better to just let things roll off your back and keep a level head for everyone else in the room, don't you think? That's the most mature way to handle it, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? Oh, no, not at all.
